| SH-18 | **Welcome splash's 4 animated stat counters replaced with a static capability strip.** John's live feedback: "tasks in progress"/"agents working"/"deliverables completed"/"$372M in spend analyzed" weren't worth showing — read as internal dashboard activity, not a reason to care, and the last one was leftover procurement-specific language contradicting the confirmed general-enterprise platform positioning (`project-deepbench` memory, S-PM-05, 2026-06-23). The real problem, in John's words: people don't understand what the agents actually do. Replaced entirely with "AUTOMATE: Market Intelligence · Data Analysis · Project Management" — three real, already-shipped capability domains (Marcus/Priya/Owen's Market Intelligence screen; Nadia Farouk's Data Expert role/`data-analysis` capability; Michelle Manning's Project Manager role), confirmed against `src/data/agents.js` before speccing, not fabricated marketing copy. Deliberately static, no live Supabase wiring — John's explicit call. Fix, 1 file, 1 task: removed the `countersRan` ref/`useEffect`, `animateCounter()`, `StatBlock`, and 4 now-dead styles (`statBlock`/`statNumber`/`statDesc`/`pulseDot`); added `capabilityRow`/`capabilityItem` styles reusing existing `pulseLabel`/`divider`. Node test 24/24 PASS (confirms new copy present, old copy/dead code fully removed, existing headline/CTA/dismiss logic untouched), build clean. **Independently re-verified by the design session, not accepted from the coding report alone:** own isolated dev server (Vite on port 5199, this session's own worktree) confirmed via the browser preview tooling — cleared `sessionStorage` and reloaded to force a fresh splash render: "AUTOMATE"/"Market Intelligence"/"Data Analysis"/"Project Management" render immediately with no counting delay, no `$` figures or old copy anywhere, headline/subhead/CTA pixel-identical, both the CTA and "×" close button correctly dismiss the splash, zero console errors, `npm run build` clean. Ran in its own git worktree (`session/splash-metrics`), zero collisions. Kickoff: `docs/kickoffs/v6.1.12-S-SPLASH-02-capability-strip.md`. | ✅ Done | S-SPLASH-02 (v6.1.12) |

| SH-11 | Restructure serverless API layer. **Fully resolved 2026-07-02 (`S-CAPABILITY-EXEC-02`):** superseded 2026-07-01 by `AA-76` (Generic Capability Executor) — the real fix wasn't consolidating routes one merge at a time, it was that capabilities shouldn't be routes at all. Both retrofits now done: `channel-intelligence.js` → `execute.js` (`S-CAPABILITY-EXEC-01`, serverless count 12→13→12), `quality-gate.js` → `execute.js` (`S-CAPABILITY-EXEC-02`, serverless count 12→11). | AI-43 | `sendRequest()`'s `latency_ms` was wrong for every capability routed through the post-v6.0.0 `execute.js` loop — its own clock restarted after the loop's `precomputed_turn` model call already happened, silently excluding the actual model-call time from the logged number. Found via a `quality-gate` smoke test (`latency_ms: 49`, physically impossible), reconfirmed live 2026-07-07 on Priya's real 27.5s hypothesis-test call (wrapper row logged `116`). Fix: `execute.js`'s already-correct `turnStart` threaded into `sendRequest()` as a new optional `turn_started_at` param — every caller that omits it (`api/plan.js`, `confirmation.js`) falls back to `Date.now()`, byte-identical to before. Independently re-verified by the design session (not accepted from the coding report alone): `git show` confirmed the exact 2-file diff; ran the Node test directly (both mocked cases PASS); a live pre/post-deploy comparison via direct Supabase query showed the same call shape jump from `latency_ms: 54` (pre-fix) to a correct `1739` (post-fix, matching its `agent-turn` sibling's `1675ms` within guardrails overhead) — and a second, independently-noticed example (Michelle's `project-manager` call, `8895` wrapper vs `8126` real turn) confirmed the fix generalizes beyond the one capability the coding session tested. | ✅ Done | S-ARCH-LATENCY-FIX-01 (79128b5) |
| AI-37 | `api/rag-query.js` was a public, unauthenticated endpoint that could read any Data Room tag by bypassing the Librarian broker. **Resolved structurally by `S-LIBRARIAN-03`** (`ARCHITECTURE.md` §19c) — Data Room content now lives in `the_Library`, a separate table whose query/write primitives live only inside `lib/librarian.js`, not exported. `api/rag-query.js` still calls `lib/rag.js`'s `queryRAG()`, which only ever targeted `knowledge_entries` — it never gained a code path to `the_Library`. Verified live 2026-07-02: probing `api/rag-query.js` with `agent_id: "apple-cso-data-room"` post-migration returns `{"context":"","chunks":[],"matchCount":0}` — the endpoint remains public (by design, for personal-training content) but the Data Room data it used to be able to leak is no longer physically reachable through it. | ✅ Done | S-LIBRARIAN-03 (c3b0d72) |

| AI-40 | `AI_TYPE_TO_SERVICE`'s `request_receivable` key (underscore, `useAIActivity.js`) never matched the actual `ai_type` value `request-receivable.js`'s `sendRequest()` wrote at its own Step 4 log (hyphen, `ai_type: 'request-receivable'`). Platform-wide, pre-existing, not introduced by `S-CAPABILITY-EXEC-01` — discovered while root-causing that session's Manual QA failure (see `AI-41`). **Resolved-as-superseded, not patched (`S-CAPABILITY-EXEC-02-design`, 2026-07-02):** `useAIActivity.js`'s "By Service" aggregation already falls back to `AI_TYPE_TO_SERVICE[ai_type] || ai_type` — an unmapped `ai_type` that already equals a `SERVICE_CATALOG` slug (which `'request-receivable'` does) resolves correctly regardless of the typo, so this was dead code, not a live bug. `AI-41`'s fix made the point moot — the literal `'request-receivable'` value is no longer written at all going forward except as an unreachable fallback. No code change made for this ID specifically. | ✅ Done (superseded, no code change) | S-CAPABILITY-EXEC-02 (4ba5bc4) |
| AI-41 | **Manual QA regression, `S-CAPABILITY-EXEC-01` (2026-07-02):** `channel-intelligence.js`'s Marcus calls used to log with specific, correctly-mapped `ai_type` values (`ci_routing`/`ci_answer`) via its own now-deleted `logActivity()` function. Retrofitted through `execute.js` → `sendRequest()`, they logged via `sendRequest()`'s generic Step 4 write instead, which hardcoded the literal `ai_type: 'request-receivable'` — losing the `ci_routing`/`ci_answer` distinction entirely. Root cause: `sendRequest()` already receives `capability_slug` as a parameter but never used it in the log write. **Fixed `S-CAPABILITY-EXEC-02` (2026-07-02):** Step 4 now logs `ai_type: capability_slug`, restoring per-capability AI Audit attribution platform-wide (not just for `channel-intelligence`/`quality-gate` — every `sendRequest()` caller, including `plan.js`, benefits). One new `AI_TYPE_TO_SERVICE` entry added (`'project-manager': 'task-planning'`) for `plan.js`'s default `capability_slug`; `channel-intelligence`/`quality-gate` needed no new entry since their `capability_slug` values already match their `SERVICE_CATALOG` slugs exactly via the existing `|| e.type` fallback. Live-verified against the deployed dev URL (not just Node test): a fresh call through `execute.js` produced a real `ai_activity_log` row with `ai_type: 'quality-gate'`, Quality Gate's By-Service call count increased, Channel Intelligence's was unaffected. 6/6 confirmable Manual QA items PASS (verified directly via Supabase + live dev URL calls); Vercel function log check (item 7) not independently verifiable this session, accepted on indirect evidence (clean 200 responses, no errors) per John's go-ahead. | ✅ Done | S-CAPABILITY-EXEC-02 (4ba5bc4) |
| AI-36 | Pattern type classification — every pattern in the registry carries a `patternType` field: `structural` (scaffolding around Claude — changes what Claude sees or when it is called, not how it reasons: RAG, Streaming, Structured Output, Embeddings, Browser Automation) or `reasoning` (changes how Claude thinks — same prompt produces meaningfully different output: ReAct, Tool Use, Prompt Chaining, Reflection, Multi-Agent Debate, Chain-of-Verification). Design requirement: (1) AI Audit By Pattern section visually distinguishes the two types — separate groupings or a type badge per row; (2) counts, cost, and availability (active/partial/roadmap) tracked and displayed per type group; (3) AiBadge labels and tooltips app-wide identify pattern type so users can see whether an element is using structural or reasoning architecture; (4) Platform Roadmap section groups roadmap patterns by type. Registry field: `patternType: 'structural' | 'reasoning'`. All 20 patterns in PATTERN_CATALOG must be classified before S-PAT-REGISTRY-01 coding begins. Depends on AI-35. Classification locked 2026-06-16: Structural (9) = RAG, Streaming, Structured Output, Embeddings, Browser Automation, Guardrails, Parallelization, HyDE, Adaptive RAG. Reasoning (11) = ReAct, Tool Use, Prompt Chaining, Reflection, HITL, Agent Orchestration, Few-Shot Prompting, LLM-as-Judge, Multi-Agent Debate, Chain-of-Verification, Episodic Memory. | ✅ Done | S-AI-PATTERN-TYPE-01 (22c9d2e) + patch (26ff072) |
| AI-39 | The Proofreader (`quality-gate`/Owen) had zero live callers anywhere on the platform since `S-APPLE-02c` built it — `S-CAPABILITY-EXEC-02` correctly deleted its standalone route (with the retry-on-block logic inside it) when generalizing every capability onto `execute.js` (`ARCHITECTURE.md` §19b), but the retry mechanism itself was never rebuilt anywhere. Confirmed live during `01b`'s QA: Marcus's self-flag alone missed the curated EMEA co-op demo case 4/4 times. **Closed by `S-MARKET-INTEL-01c`:** `runQaWithQualityGate()` in `MarketIntelligenceScreen.jsx` rebuilds the retry-once-on-block mechanism as a Layer 2 orchestration (two ordinary `execute.js` calls — Marcus, then Owen), and computes `needs_review` as Marcus's self-flag OR Owen's independent `eval.result==='revise'`, per the locked design doc §5.2 rule. Also fixed a real gap in the original design: `quality-gate`'s `task_context` never included the original question, which `qg-review-intent`'s `responsiveness` scoring dimension requires — now included. **Root cause found during the coding session's own Category L test, not a defect in the shipped screen code:** `qg-review-intent`'s Supabase `max_tokens` was `600`, truncating the `eval` object on every real call before it could complete (same failure shape as `01b`'s `hyp-generation-intent` truncation bug) — raised to `1500`. Node test (categories A/B/C/G, including a pure-function retry simulation covering block-then-pass and double-block per `STANDARDS.md` Section 4's retry-logic-test requirements) and Category L (3 live calls against the real deployed capability) both PASS. **Independently re-verified by the design session directly against the live deployed dev URL** (not accepted from the coding report alone): a real `ci-answer-intent` answer that Marcus self-flagged `needs_review:false` was blocked by Owen for `hallucinated_internal_data`; the retry-once regenerated the answer, passed guardrail, but `eval.result:'revise'` still correctly flagged it — full end-to-end proof of the two-layer OR-gate and retry-once mechanism working exactly as designed, live, on real production data (not a synthetic test fixture). Kickoff: `docs/kickoffs/v6.0.20-S-MARKET-INTEL-01c-proofreader-pipeline-log.md`. | ✅ Done | S-MARKET-INTEL-01c-design/S-MARKET-INTEL-01c (v6.0.20, 7952395/2282513) |
| AI-50 | AI Audit's "By Pattern" and MI screen's Agent Routing subline both rolled up cost/patterns from `SERVICE_CATALOG`'s static per-*service* tags, not real per-call data — confirmed ~3x cost overcount (\$62.10 vs \$21.03 Total Cost). Fixed across 4 sessions same day (2026-07-14): **`AI-50a`** (v6.2.23, `6879225`) threaded each Intent Skill Profile's real `technical_services` through `db-assembly.js`→`ai-enrichment.js`→`request-receivable.js` into `ai_activity_log.patterns_used`, no schema change. **`AI-50a2`** (v6.2.26, `23e4ba0`) extended the same merge fix to `execute.js`'s `logAgentTurn()`, a second independent write site `AI-50a` missed — pure-delegation intents (`ci-answer-display-intent`, `hyp-hypothesis-test-display-intent`) never reached `sendRequest()` and always logged mechanical-only data until this fix. **`AI-50b`** (Supabase content only, no version) populated `technical_services` for the 5 remaining empty Intent Skill Profiles and gated `hyp-knowledge`'s RAG to only the 2 intents that use it. **`AI-50c`** (v6.2.28, `8910857`) rewrote `useAIActivity.js`'s `byPattern` as a testable `computeByPattern()` (real per-call pattern attribution, cost split evenly across genuinely-simultaneous patterns per call) and `MarketIntelligenceScreen.jsx`'s Agent Routing subline to read real `evt.data.patterns_used`; fixed 2 catalog gaps (`'reflection'`→`'reflect'` slug rename, added `'intelligent-synthesis'`). Each session live-verified against real production data/deployment, not just Node tests — `AI-50c`'s own check fed 200 real rows through the shipped `computeByPattern()` and confirmed bucket-sum cost reconciles exactly (to the fraction of a cent) to the sum of entries with real pattern data, closing the overcount bug structurally. Known deliberate scope boundary: `execute.js`'s `resumeCapability()` checkpoint/resume path still falls back to mechanical-only pattern detection — not fixed by this effort. Full narrative for all 4 sessions: `docs/SESSIONS.md`. | ✅ Done | S-AI-50a-design/S-AI-50a (v6.2.23, `6879225`) ✅ — S-AI-50a2 (v6.2.26, `23e4ba0`) ✅ — S-AI-50b ✅ — S-AI-50c (v6.2.28, `8910857`) ✅ |
| AI-51 | **Found 2026-07-14, John reviewing the live AI Audit screen right after `AI-50c` shipped.** Two issues: By Pattern's bucket sum ran ~\$4-6 short of Total Cost (root-caused as mostly-expected content-coverage backlog, matches `AI-50c`'s own design) — but investigating it surfaced a genuine, quantified double-count bug: `execute.js`'s `logAgentTurn()` writes a row for every turn, `sendRequest()` writes a second row for the same turn when terminal, both carrying identical tokens/`patterns_used` — both were being priced and both counted toward `computeByPattern()`'s per-pattern totals. Live-quantified: 1,107 paired `agent-turn` rows, \$5.58 double-counted out of \$22.82 Total Cost (~24% inflation). A blanket "zero all agent-turn cost" fix would be wrong — pure delegating turns have no paired wrapper row and are the sole record of that spend. Fix reused `useAgents.js`'s already-proven-live `S-MI-20` pairing check (`CAPABILITY_WRAPPER_TYPES`/`PAIR_WINDOW_MS`, built for latency dedup) applied to cost + pattern-count instead — moved to `useAIActivity.js` as the canonical shared module, new exported `pairedAgentTurnIds()`, wired into both `hydrateFromSupabase()` and `useAgents.js`'s `buildActivitySummary()`. Also fixed `AIActivityPanel.jsx`'s "Services Active" stat hardcoding a stale `/14` denominator (`SERVICE_CATALOG` has 32 real entries today) via a new `servicesCatalogTotal` field. 3 files, Node test 5/5 PASS (coding session also caught and fixed a real gap beyond the kickoff's literal spec: `useAgentActivitySummary()`'s Supabase `.select()` was missing `id`, which would have made every row share `id: undefined` and falsely pair the whole batch). **Design session's own live verification against real production data:** replicated the shipped pairing/cost logic directly in SQL against all 6,868 real rows — confirmed Total Cost drops from \$23.68 to \$18.53 (removing \$5.15 across 1,027 paired rows), while a genuine pure-delegation `agent-turn` row with no paired wrapper (real tokens, id 9746) correctly retains its real cost, proving both directions of the fix. Kickoff: `docs/kickoffs/v6.2.34-AI-51-agent-turn-double-count-and-service-count-fix.md`. | ✅ Done | S-AI-51-design/S-AI-51 (v6.2.34, `b828422`) ✅ |
| AI-49 | **Found 2026-07-13 while pulling live data for `AI-48`'s design mock:** every row in the most recent 500-row `ai_activity_log` window had `cost_usd = 0`/`null`, including real LLM calls with real token counts. **Root-caused and fixed 2026-07-14 (`S-AA-181-design`/`S-AA-181`, tracked as `AA-183` — see that row in the AGENT ARCHITECTURE — AA section below for the ID-collision note):** every server-side write site logs `input_tokens`/`output_tokens` but never computes `cost_usd`; fixed at read time via a shared `computeCallCost()` using real split input/output Anthropic pricing, no schema change. Full detail: `AA-183` (below). | ✅ Done | S-AA-181-design/S-AA-181 (v6.2.19, 042a5ba) |
| AI-47 | Two distinct `ai_activity_log` token-write bugs, root-caused 2026-07-08 while trying to root-cause `AA-151` (a shipped `max_tokens` fix hadn't moved the needle, and there was no real token data to check the theory against). Bug 1: `execute.js`'s `logAgentTurn()` (the delegation-loop's per-turn write) had no token parameter in its signature at all — `turn.usage` was available at its call site and simply never passed through. Bug 2: `request-receivable.js`'s `sendRequest()` STEP 4 wrote a number but mislabeled it — stuffed the combined input+output total into `input_tokens`, never wrote `output_tokens`. Fixed both (2 files), Node test 2/2, build clean. Live-verified directly by the design session against the deployed dev URL (not accepted from the coding report alone) — confirmed real, correctly-labeled `input_tokens`/`output_tokens` now populate on fresh calls through both write sites, including `agent-selection-intent`'s own turns, which is what actually unblocked `AA-151`'s real diagnosis (see that row: budget theory subsequently ruled out by the real data this fix produced). | ✅ Done | S-ARCH-AI-ACTIVITY-TOKENS-01 (v6.1.33) |

Streamed via opt-in `stream:true` SSE, reusing `api/plan.js`'s proven `text/event-stream` idiom verbatim (`streamResult()` helper) — every non-streamed caller (`test-durable-loop-retrofit.js`, `test-s-arch-hitl-resume-01a.mjs`) gets byte-identical plain JSON, confirmed unaffected. Client-side (`MarketIntelligenceScreen.jsx`): `readSSEResult()` mirrors `CreateWorkOrderScreen.jsx`'s existing reader; live delegation events and the pre-existing scripted macro-hop swaps both feed one shared `setStatus()`/`workingStatus`, two-tone styled (`kind: 'scripted'` vs `'orchestration'`) so a viewer can tell "this always happens" from "an agent just decided this." Final receipt: `"Full Agent Routing & Answer Given in Xs"` caption on the answer bubble (`msg.totalElapsedMs`, a simple end-start diff). Also fixed 2 pre-existing `SERVICE_CATALOG` gaps found along the way (`channel-intelligence` missing `Agent Delegation` despite `AA-164` already using it) and broadened the `streaming` `PATTERN_CATALOG` description to honestly cover event-granularity, not just token-granularity. **Forward-compatible with `AA-171`:** the guardrail-block path's hardcoded `agent_id:"sam"` call bypasses the harness loop entirely (screen-scripted), so it's structurally invisible to this mechanism today — correctly gets only the plain scripted styling until `AA-171` routes it through real agent reasoning, at which point it lands on an already-instrumented dispatch point automatically, zero new code. | MI-44 | **Work-dropdown click-interception hotfix.** John's direct live bug report right after `S-MI-32`/`MI-36` shipped: dropdown items didn't respond to clicks, first click on "Work" appeared to do nothing. Root-caused via `elementFromPoint` hit-testing, not guessed: the click-outside backdrop (`zIndex:998`) is a sibling of the nav wrapper, but that wrapper has `transform:translateX(-50%)`, which creates its own CSS stacking context — the dropdown's own `zIndex:999` is only compared *inside* that context, so from the backdrop's side the whole wrapper had no z-index at all, and the backdrop (998) painted and captured clicks above the entire Work tab and dropdown. Fixed with one added `zIndex:1000` on the nav wrapper. Live-verified the full repro sequence (fresh load → click Work → click Bench → click Work again → click a dashboard item) end to end, matching exactly what was reported. Also added the requested small gold (`T.brassLight`) down-arrow dropdown hint (▼, 7px, non-interactive). Node test 3/3 PASS, build clean. Renumbered `MI-42`→`43`→`44` (`v6.1.43`→`44`→`45`) during rebase — 2 other concurrent same-day sessions claimed those IDs/versions first, resolved cleanly (no file-region overlap). No formal kickoff doc — direct root-cause investigation and fix by the design session itself, given the live-bug-report context. | ✅ Done | S-MI-44 (v6.1.45, hotfix) |

| MI-58 | **Fix "expect > Xs" estimate depth-blindness.** John's direct live-reported bug (screenshot: "expect > 30s" badge sitting far below real elapsed time, 53s and counting). Root-caused live against real `ai_activity_log` data, not guessed: `estimateChainMs()` summed one blended `avgLatency` per intent across the `qa` chain (routing→answer→review→display), treating each as exactly one LLM turn — but `execute.js`'s `:depthN` tag showed `ci-answer-intent`/`qg-review-intent` commonly loop through 2-3 real turns before a single frontend call returns (e.g. `ci-answer-intent` reached depth1 on ~69% of calls), so the blended single-hop average structurally undercounted the real per-call cost. Confirmed with John before coding (two explicit judgment calls, not assumed): use **p75 per depth** rather than the mean (a more conservative "expect >" floor), and add a **30-day recency window** (confirmed live this is a no-op today — all 4 qa-chain intents' data only goes back to 2026-07-07, matters going forward as history accumulates). **Fix, 2 files, additive only:** `useAgents.js`'s `buildActivitySummary()` gains a per-depth `byDepth` breakdown (p75 + calls per depth) alongside the existing `avgLatency`/`maxLatency`/`calls`/`byModel` fields, which stay byte-identical — the Agents drawer needed zero code changes. `estimateChainMs()` rewritten to sum each hop's depth0 p75 plus every further depth's p75 weighted by how often that depth is actually reached (expected-value-per-call instead of expected-value-per-turn); `formatExpectation()`/all 3 call sites unchanged. Node test 8/8 PASS, build clean, `git status` confirmed exactly the 2 intended files touched. **Design session's own live verification against the real deployed dev preview, not accepted from the coding report alone:** ran the identical EMEA co-op-budget question from John's original screenshot — initial "expect < 2m" ceiling still showed immediately on submit; once routing resolved, the badge upgraded to "expect > 1m 5s" (vs. the old ~25-30s), and the real run (which happened to hit a self-flag→guardrail-block escalation path deeper than the standard 4-hop chain) completed in 75.0s, confirming the new estimate is now in the right neighborhood instead of off by 2x+. Also confirmed a real production hop the fix's diagnosis predicted: Owen's quality gate genuinely routed back to Marcus mid-run for a retry, exactly the loop the old estimate never accounted for. Agents drawer spot-checked after a **fresh page reload at 1280×800** (not a live resize — same `matchMedia`-on-mount tooling quirk already documented by `MI-50`/`MI-51`, confirmed again here) — all `byKind` avg/max/calls rows rendered identically to their pre-fix shape and values, no regression from the additive `byDepth` field. Zero console errors throughout. Kickoff: `docs/kickoffs/v6.2.29-MI-58-expected-time-estimate-fix.md`. | ✅ Done | S-MI-58-design/S-MI-58 (v6.2.29, 781a4b7) |

Marcus then finishes his own answer with a real `confidence_tier: "sourced"`, citing Eleanor's actual `the_library` row ids. Zero changes to `ci-routing-intent`'s taxonomy or Owen's guardrail logic — the fix works by never letting Marcus produce an `na`-tagged catalog answer in the first place. 3 files (`lib/librarian.js`, `lib/search-harness.js`, `api/prompt/ai-enrichment.js`) + 2 new Skill Profiles (`eleanor-catalog-knowledge`, `library-catalog-intent`) + a method-text carve-out on `ci-answer-intent`. **Root-cause bug found and fixed live during the coding session's own Category L testing** (not in the original kickoff): Marcus's `delegate_to_agent` call to Eleanor was setting `is_final: true`, terminating the loop with Eleanor's raw `{answer, citations}` object (no `confidence_tier`) instead of returning to Marcus to compose his own sourced answer — fixed by extending the same in-scope `ci-answer-intent` method-text field with an explicit `is_final: false` instruction for this hand-off. Node test 29/29 PASS, including the definitive repro-and-fix pair: L3 called `ci-answer-intent` with John's exact original question and confirmed `confidence_tier: "sourced"` (not `na`) with real, verified `the_library` citations; L4 fed that answer through `qg-review-intent` directly and confirmed the guardrail no longer blocks. Independently re-verified by the design session (not accepted from the coding report alone): live Supabase query confirmed `ci-answer-intent.method` contains both the Eleanor Voss carve-out and the mid-session `is_final` fix; a fresh `ai_activity_log` query independently traced the full real multi-hop chain (Marcus → Michelle's `agent-selection-intent` → Eleanor's `librarian`/`library-catalog-intent` calls → Marcus's final turn → Owen's `qg-review-intent`) with real timestamps, confirming the delegation loop genuinely executed end to end, not fabricated. Deliberately deferred, logged, not built this session: a dedicated Pipeline Log case for Eleanor's own turn (cosmetic-only, would be a 4th file); adding `'Agent Delegation'` to `channel-intelligence`'s `SERVICE_CATALOG` patterns (separate, already-logged catalog-accuracy gap); Owen's `qg-review-intent` still has no written rubric for `confidence_tier: na` in general — this fix sidesteps that gap for this specific question class rather than resolving it platform-wide. Kickoff: `docs/kickoffs/v6.1.32-S-ARCH-LIBRARIAN-CATALOG-01-aa162-eleanor-catalog-intent.md`. | ✅ Done | S-ARCH-LIBRARIAN-CATALOG-01-design/S-ARCH-LIBRARIAN-CATALOG-01 (v6.1.32, 0dcc8fe) |

| AA-118 | **Real per-hop `latency_ms` logging + narrowed Michelle's `agent-selection-intent` — fixes the `ci-answer-display-intent` 504.** Diagnosed 2026-07-06 without any new instrumentation — existing `ai_activity_log` data, grouped by timestamp gap, cross-referenced against a live Vercel log export: every 504 hit almost exactly 60,000-60,261ms, deterministic, not a rare slow call. Root cause: `librarian`/`agent-directory`/`guardrails-check` all logged `latency_ms: null` (the already-open `AI-43` gap, closed here as a side effect), and Michelle's own `agent-selection-intent` call — the actual slowest single hop, ~20s — had no cap in its `objective` on how many of the 21 roster agents to rate, inheriting the platform default `max_tokens: 4000`. Fix: `logLibrarianCall`/`logLibrarianWrite` (`lib/librarian.js`), `logDirectoryCall` (`lib/project-manager.js`), and the guardrails-check block (`api/prompt/request-receivable.js`) now compute and log real elapsed time instead of omitting/hardcoding `null`; `agent-selection-intent`'s Supabase row narrowed to "top 3-5 genuinely relevant candidates" and `max_tokens` 4000→1200 (schema unchanged, model unchanged — a verbosity cap, not a capability downgrade). A client-side retry was explicitly ruled out first (would just 504 again, every time, for a deterministic-not-rare timeout) and two bigger architectural options (frontend-exposed loop state via HTTP-split, or a background-job/polling pattern) were considered and deliberately set aside as bigger, separate work if this trim didn't fully solve it. Node test PASS, build clean. **Independently re-verified by the design session, not accepted from the coding report alone:** `git show` confirmed the diff matches the kickoff exactly, no scope creep, every call site updated; direct Supabase query confirmed `agent-selection-intent`'s `objective`/`max_tokens` updated and schema (`candidates`/`reasoning`) unchanged; a fresh, independent live call against the deployed dev function (not the coding session's own test) completed in 28.4 seconds — genuine Michelle reasoning narrowed to 2 real candidates (not hardcoded), Alex selected on merit — versus the deterministic 60-60.2s/504 pattern before this session; a follow-up direct query of `ai_activity_log` confirmed every row logged after the deploy (21:55:17 UTC onward) carries a real, non-null `latency_ms` (`librarian` 230-714ms, `agent-directory` 101-125ms, `guardrails-check` 633-2624ms, `project-manager` 747-2740ms) — rows from before the deploy finished propagating correctly still show `null`, as expected, not a lingering bug. `AA-116` (genuine routing for AI - Hypothesis Test) is sequenced to build on this now-shipped fix, not before it. | ✅ Done | S-ARCH-LOOP-LATENCY-01-design/S-ARCH-LOOP-LATENCY-01 (v6.0.26, 3b6d8ab) |

Resolved explicitly as plain `user`, not "HITL User" — HITL is a distinct, already-used platform term for Bench/Work-Order step gates (`AboutPanel.jsx` glossary, `PATTERN_CATALOG`'s `hitl` entry), and "director" covered plain conversational actions too, not just review-gate moments. Supabase `hyp-stress-test-intent` → `hyp-hypothesis-test-intent` (`skill_profiles` + `capability_skill_profiles`, coding session resolved a non-deferrable FK constraint not anticipated in the kickoff by dropping and re-adding it, validated clean afterward). | AA-113 | **`callModel()` retries once on a transient non-2xx Anthropic HTTP error, instead of failing immediately with zero trace.** Originally found 2026-07-04 (`S-MARKET-INTEL-01d-design`'s own QA: an Accept on a `pending_confirmation` 500'd once, succeeded cleanly on an immediate manual retry, exactly one `the_reasoning` row written, no duplicate). Never scheduled until re-surfaced 2026-07-07 while investigating a second live "Something went wrong reaching Marcus" report, distinct from `AA-120` (already shipped): John confirmed the symptom occurs even when Owen's quality-gate retry path never fires, ruling out a chain-specific latency explanation. Checked directly rather than assumed: the failed `ci-answer-display-intent` call's own `ai_activity_log` trail showed only ~21s of logged activity before going silent (nowhere near the 60s ceiling), and `MarketIntelligenceScreen.jsx`'s `callCapability()` has no client-side timeout of its own — ruling out both a latency-ceiling cause and a client-side-abort cause. This left `callModel()`'s complete absence of retry on a transient Anthropic error (shared by every agent's every turn, at every depth of the delegation loop) as the best fit: a single bad API response anywhere in the chain throws immediately, with zero trace, since `AA-120`'s `logAgentTurn()` only logs a turn that already succeeded. **Fix, 2 files:** `request-receivable.js` gains `postToAnthropicWithRetry()` — retries once, with a 1s backoff, on `429`/`500`/`502`/`503`/`529` only; any other status (a malformed request, an auth failure) still fails fast, unchanged, since retrying those would never help. Wraps only the initial call in `callModel()` — the existing, separate parse-failure retry (a schema-conformance issue, not an HTTP error) is deliberately left untouched. `execute.js`'s `logAgentTurn()` (from `AA-120`) gains an `api_retry_count` param, surfaced as a `:apiRetryN` suffix on the logged `feature` string, so a retry is directly visible in `ai_activity_log` without needing raw Vercel logs. Node test 14/14 PASS (Category B source checks; Category G mocked transient-529-then-success and non-transient-400-fails-fast cases, deterministic rather than waiting for a real API hiccup; Category L live call), build clean. One test-script-only issue found and fixed along the way, not a code bug: the kickoff's own regex for "`callModel()` uses the retry helper" anchored on the first literal occurrence of the string "callModel" with too small a character budget — tightened to anchor on the actual `export async function callModel(` declaration; source files unchanged. **Independently re-verified by the design session, not accepted from the coding report alone:** `git diff` confirmed the exact 2-file change, parse-failure retry path byte-identical; ran the Node test directly (14/14 PASS, not just the coding report's claim); ran `npm run build` directly (910 modules, 3.43s, zero errors); two separate fresh live calls against the deployed dev URL (not the coding session's own test) — one immediately after push (still serving pre-deploy code, useful only as a before/after baseline), one after the deploy had propagated — both HTTP 200 with a clean `final_delegation`; a direct `ai_activity_log` query after the propagated call confirmed the new `agent-turn` logging shape working exactly as designed (`channel-intelligence:ci-answer-display-intent:depth0`/`depth1`, no `:apiRetry` suffix on this clean run, consistent with `apiRetryCount: 0`). **Separate finding from this same investigation, not fixed by this session:** Owen's `qg-review-intent` retry-to-Marcus delegation — confirmed architecturally compliant with the self-routing rule (Owen is handed the originating agent's identity via `task_context`, never a static name) — can still chain his own two evaluation turns plus Marcus's full regeneration to ~50s inside one 60s-budgeted request. A genuine, separate latency risk, left open pending a structural-fix-vs-trim decision with John. | ✅ Done | S-ARCH-API-RETRY-01-design/S-ARCH-API-RETRY-01 (v6.0.30, bfce649) |
| AA-108 | **Silent-fabrication bug, fixed.** Michelle's `agent-selection-intent` was hallucinating `intent_slug` values — its schema required one per candidate, but `lib/project-manager.js`'s roster broker only ever supplied `capability_slug`, giving the model no ground truth. Found live during `S-ARCH-HITL-RESUME-02`'s Category L test (2026-07-03, the first live test to ever exercise `request_help`→`delegate_to_agent` through to a capability needing a specific intent): the model guessed a plausible but nonexistent slug (`library-write-execution` instead of the real `library-write-intent`), and `db-assembly.js`'s `assemblePrompt()` silently filtered to zero Intent Skill Profiles instead of erroring — so the downstream call proceeded with no schema/handler, and Eleanor's model turn free-wrote a fully fabricated, realistic "✅ INSERTED" confirmation. Independently confirmed by direct query: zero `the_library` rows existed anywhere in the entire test window, despite every layer reporting success. **Fix, two parts:** (1) `lib/project-manager.js`'s `fetchRoster()`/`rosterToContext()` now join `capability_skill_profiles`+`skill_profiles` to surface each capability's real intent slugs in the roster context, generically, for every agent — same mechanism `capability_slug` already used, no hardcoded values anywhere; `agent-selection-intent` got explicit instructions to copy exactly or return `null`, never guess. (2) `assemblePrompt()` now throws a clear, capability-and-slug-naming error when a caller passes an `intent_slug` that doesn't match any loaded Intent Skill Profile, instead of silently degrading — the platform's "fail safe, never fake" principle applied structurally, same class as `resolveCapabilityHolder()`'s existing unhandled throws. **Conclusive live proof:** re-ran `S-ARCH-HITL-RESUME-02`'s exact Escalate propose→accept scenario end to end — this time it landed a real, uniquely-marked `the_library` row (not a loose filter match), independently re-verified directly against Supabase by the design session using a fresh script with a different deliberately-wrong `intent_slug` than the original bug, confirming the guard is generic, not special-cased. Root cause: the `final_delegation` shape (`execute.js`, the `is_final` branch) spread the delegate's real content flat at the top level with no `.content` key at all, while the guard checked `delegateResult.content` — correct for the ordinary `sendRequest()` shape, but reads `undefined` when `delegateResult` is itself a nested `final_delegation` object, throwing "returned no content" even though real content existed. **Fix, 1 file, 3 tasks:** extracted `buildFinalDelegationResult()` (new named export in `execute.js`) from the inline `is_final` block — the one behavioral change is that it now always mirrors real content under a `.content` key alongside the existing flat spread, making the shape self-describing at any recursion depth; the empty-content guard itself is unchanged (still throws on genuine no-content). Node.js unit test (4 cases, including a direct nested-`final_delegation`-as-`delegateResult` regression case) confirms the fix logically; Category L live test against the 3 real seed questions directly observed the fix firing on a genuine `final_delegation` result. **Independently re-verified by the design session against the real deployed dev URL, not accepted from the coding report alone:** all 3 seed questions confirmed working live via `claude-in-chrome` — Vietnam (rendered via `AA-137`'s existing decline/`NEEDS REVIEW` fallback, not a crash), EMEA (hit `AA-141`'s already-known transient `continue` 500 on the first attempt, self-resolved cleanly on retry with a real 2-hop Marcus→Michelle→Alex delegation, correct `confidence_tier`), Japan (routed to the Theory flow, correct intent classification). Zero unexpected console/network errors across all runs. Honest caveat carried from the coding session: a true two-level-deep nested `is_final` chain was not observed live (not reliably forceable on demand) — the Node.js test is the deterministic proof of the fix itself. New finding from the coding session's own live test spun out to its own row (`AA-145`, not left buried here): intermittent genuinely-empty-content case on `alex`/`screen-controls`/`qa-answer-format`, correctly caught by the unweakened guard. Ran in the `mi-loop-reliability` worktree, zero collisions. | AA-139 | Retrofit of `AA-138`'s POC into the real `runCapability()` loop, John's go-ahead 2026-07-07. Hybrid design: `runCapability()` stays fully in-process/synchronous by default (zero added latency for the common case); a shared `runLoop()` helper checks remaining wall-clock against the shared `maxDuration: 60` budget before each hop — only checkpoints to `durable_hops` and returns `{status:'in_progress', job_id}` when a hop risks the ceiling. `_deadline` threaded through nested calls exactly like the existing `_hop_counter` pattern, so the shared ceiling stays real across delegation hops. POC's duplicate loop (`lib/durable-loop-poc.js`) retired per `ARCHITECTURE.md` §19b. Split into 2 sub-sessions (exceeded 3-file/4-task budget, 2 layers): `S-ARCH-DURABLE-LOOP-02a` — harness (`runLoop()` extraction, `resumeCapability()`, new `action:'continue'` dispatch). `S-ARCH-DURABLE-LOOP-02b` — frontend (`MarketIntelligenceScreen.jsx`'s shared `resolveInProgress()` helper wired into `callCapability()`'s 9 call sites and `resolveConfirmation()`'s 1). | AA-147 | **Formerly tracked informally as `task_a8249f13`; given its own row 2026-07-08 at the start of `S-ARCH-SCHEMA-GUARD-01-design` (Backlog Capture rule), tagged `Continuity` under the new Type taxonomy.** `buildCallBody()`'s `tool_choice` is forced to `'auto'` whenever a capability has both a JSON schema and `can_request_help: true` — necessary so the model can legitimately choose `request_help`/`delegate_to_agent` instead of answering directly, but `'auto'` also let the model satisfy "call a tool" by calling its own schema tool with an empty or partial input object. `parseModelTurn()` only checked that *a* `tool_use` block existed, never that its `input` held the schema's required fields; the unvalidated input flowed straight through `sendRequest()` into a deliverable with missing/undefined content, no error anywhere. **Confirmed blast radius via live Supabase query before speccing the fix:** 10 Intent Skill Profiles combine `can_request_help`+schema; 2 (`ci-answer-display-intent`, `hyp-hypothesis-test-display-intent`) were already protected by `AA-142`'s guard, leaving 8 unprotected — including `ci-answer-intent` (Marcus's main Q&A path) and `qg-review-intent` (Owen's Proofreader, runs on every Q&A/hypothesis turn), the widest-blast-radius unfixed gap in the `AA-126`/`AA-142`/`AA-144`/`AA-141`/`AA-145`/`AA-135` continuity bug family. **Fix, 1 file:** `parseModelTurn()` now takes a `schemaTool` param and throws (naming the missing keys) when the model's own schema-tool call is missing required fields; both `callModel()` call sites updated to pass it through. Reuses `callModel()`'s existing retry-once-then-throw path unchanged — no new retry logic. `request_help`/`delegate_to_agent` calls are explicitly exempt. Live Q&A round trip against the real deployed dev URL (coding session's own test, one pass per `STANDARDS.md`'s live-test-once rule) exercised both newly-protected high-traffic intents at once — `ci-answer-intent` (Answered, confidence_tier: Sourced, 25.8s) and `qg-review-intent` (Owen's guardrail/eval: pass, 9.4s) — full loop completed cleanly, all 4 `/api/capabilities/execute` calls 200, zero console errors. One display artifact observed (Riley's HTML rendering as raw text) was confirmed pre-existing and unrelated (`AA-135`/`AA-146` territory — `schemaTool` is null on the `html` output-type path, so this fix's new branch never engages there), not a regression. | AA-126 | **Silent content-loss bug on the `delegate_to_agent` hand-off — root-caused and fixed 2026-07-07, found by the design session investigating John's "haven't seen Column 2 charts in several sessions" question.** Root cause: `DELEGATE_TO_AGENT_TOOL`'s `task` field (`request-receivable.js`) is a plain string by design — the delegating agent (Priya) had to paraphrase her own structured `supports`/`complicates`/`consider` content (with real citation UUIDs) into free text for that one field. | AA-148 | **Live-reproduced 2026-07-08 from John's own report** (a Q&A answer that was narration about a delegation instead of real content, with Michelle/Alex missing from Agent Routing) — confirmed via `claude-in-chrome` against the real dev URL and cross-checked against `ai_activity_log`/`durable_hops`, not accepted from the screenshot alone. **Not a recurrence of `AA-142`** — `delegation_required: true` was correctly set and real delegation genuinely occurred (Michelle really selected a Display agent, the agent really generated real output). Two compounding gaps, both confirmed live: (1) `ci-answer-display-intent`/`hyp-hypothesis-test-display-intent` left `is_final` as model judgment on `delegate_to_agent` even though both intents' own method text guarantees there's never a legitimate `false` case (a Format Skill never changes what it's handed) — when the model omitted `is_final:true`, Marcus got a superfluous wrap-up turn that narrated instead of relaying the delegate's real content, and `display.selection`/`display_agent_id` never populated (why Michelle/Alex were invisible in Agent Routing — a downstream symptom, not a separate logging bug). (2) That narration should still have been caught by `AA-142`'s guard, but `resumeCapability()` hardcoded `delegationRequired: false` on any request that checkpoints mid-flight — an already-documented `AA-142` "accepted gap" that live `durable_hops` data proved was not hypothetical: this exact reproduction's request checkpointed and resumed mid-flight, silently disabling the guard for the rest of the request. **Fix, 2 code files + 1 Supabase migration:** `execute.js`'s terminal-delegation branch changed to `if (delegationRequired || turn.tool_input.is_final === true)` — no legitimate `false` case exists once a capability's entire job is delegation, same logic `delegation_required` itself already established (§19d sniff test resolved explicitly in the kickoff, not just cited); `durable_hops` gained a `delegation_required` column, threaded through `checkpointAndReturn()`/`createDurableHopRow()`/`resumeCapability()` so a resumed chain keeps the guard active. No data/prompt edit needed — both intents' `skill_profiles.traits.delegation_required` were already correct. Node test 6/6 PASS, including a **deterministic** forced checkpoint/resume case via the existing `__setTestBudgetMs()` test hook (not dependent on random narration timing, unlike `AA-142`'s own test). **Independently re-verified by the design session, not accepted from the coding report alone:** the coding session's own live QA (all 6 Manual QA items) exercised a real production checkpoint/resume for real (`durable_hops` row `d4d18e19...`, two `depth1` turns 12.8s apart) and confirmed the correct final result. The design session then ran its own separate live spot-check via `claude-in-chrome` against the same dev URL: run 1 hit a genuine, already-documented, pre-existing intermittent case (`AA-144`/`AA-145`'s "empty content from `alex`/`screen-controls`/`qa-answer-format`, correctly caught by the unweakened guard") — surfaced as a clean error now instead of being masked by narration, which is the fix working correctly, not a regression; run 2 succeeded cleanly, Michelle→Riley Torres delegation with full real HTML-formatted content (citations, driver table) and correct 7-event Agent Routing attribution. Non-block path confirmed structurally unaffected — the entire change is contained inside the existing `if (gate.guardrail?.result === "block")` branch, everything outside it byte-identical per `git show`.
